# Timeline, station and map sources

Research register assembled **23 September 2026** for the eight systems then in the repository; the Chengdu, Beijing, Nanjing and Chongqing sections were added on **24 September 2026** from new research.

This file preserves references recovered from earlier research and adds newly located corroboration. It indexes **every current event entry and every legend line**, including historical predecessors, trams, light rail and gondolas. It also records sources for station/transfer identities, kilometres and drawn geography.

The Tokyo, Shenzhen, Hangzhou and Guangzhou line-history references were largely recovered from earlier map-building research. Most Hong Kong, Shanghai, Taipei, Singapore and Foshan line-history links were located again for this register; they should not be represented as a recovered original bibliography. The existing spec also preserved several original Hong Kong and geographic references.

**Coverage is not the same as verification.** Some entries still have only a secondary chronology, some exact days are placeholders, and some historical distances/coordinates are estimates. Those limitations are recorded below; this is not a claim that every existing value has a reliable primary citation. No timeline data or geometry was changed while assembling this register.

## How to read and maintain this file

- **Recovered** means the reference was found in the existing spec, earlier research files or their bibliographies. It does not mean the original author of every asset can be proven to have used it.
- **New** means a reference located for this register; it corroborates existing data rather than proving its original provenance.
- **Recovered citation** links preserve useful original notices and contemporary reports cited by the earlier research. They have not all been independently reopened; a link is not a claim of successful retrieval. Archives and WeChat in particular may block automated access.
- **Secondary chronology** means a line/station history, usually Wikipedia. It is useful for locating dated primary evidence, but must not settle a disputed day merely because the repo agrees with it.
- **Unresolved / unverified day** identifies a specific claim that still needs evidence. A source for a line opening does not automatically substantiate a rename, delayed station or paid-area change listed in the same event.
- Event-table descriptions below are an inventory copied from the repository, **not quotations from the linked sources**. Sources apply only to their stated subject. The table deliberately preserves questionable entries so they can be corrected in the data after verification.
- Prefer operator notices and dated maps; then government records, contemporary reporting and scholarly histories; then secondary timelines and their footnotes. A planned opening, trial without public passengers, construction completion or ceremony is not necessarily first passenger service.

### What needs a source

| Stored fact                                                       | Evidence to use                                                                                                                             |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `events.json`: each date and each description                     | Event index below; resolve each independent claim, not just the first description on the date.                                              |
| `lines.json`: historical names and start/end dates                | Line histories and dated naming notices; corporate establishment, service takeover and public renaming can occur on different days.         |
| SVG track labels: opening, removal, reassignment                  | Opening/withdrawal notices and historical route diagrams; end dates are exclusive, so first absent day may be the day after last operation. |
| SVG station labels: name, opening, relocation, marker replacement | Line station tables plus station-specific histories and operator plans; a marker upgrade is not a newly opened physical station.            |
| SVG `data-lines`, connectors and station identity                 | Dated interchange diagrams and fare-area notices. Walking connection, continuous fare and shared paid area are different facts.             |
| SVG `data-km`                                                     | Published operating section length, with rounding/allocation documented; not the length of the Bézier path.                                 |
| SVG route/station/water coordinates                               | Original map provenance, engineering alignment and mapped geographic features; modern geometry does not establish historical geometry.      |
| Colours, logos and compact interchange shapes                     | Operator wayfinding/map and original artwork. These are presentation choices, not extra historical events.                                  |

The date boundaries in the indexed `map.svg` files (station/track labels and `data-lines`) and their `lines.json` legends were compared with their `events.json`: **none fall on a date absent from the event index**. This checks coverage of dates, not correctness or completeness of every description. Source links for the line station tables also cover intermediate stations omitted from event prose.

Use [the map data spec](map-data-spec.md#11-sources-and-verification) for scope and parsing. This is a build history: maintenance, accidents and temporary operating suspensions normally do not erase built track. Public previews count in several existing systems; the inconsistent treatment in Singapore/Taipei needs an explicit decision, documented below.

## Accuracy gaps to keep visible

1. **Hong Kong:** 1913 Sheung Shui halt and other early KCR/Sha Tau Kok halts are not fully inventoried; Hung Hom 1921 closure, Kowloon Tong 1982 opening and 1996 names need exact-day evidence. Several 1960s rename dates encode only a known year. Historical KCR geometry and Light Rail stage lengths are estimates.
2. **Shanghai:** early surface-alignment replacements, Maglev first public service, paid-area passage dates and exact English naming rollouts need stronger primary evidence. The English timeline and other histories disagree on the first Line 3 day.
3. **Taipei:** extension dates do not by themselves prove when modern combined line names became official. Some stored section kilometres differ from operator historical tables. Circular Line trial rides also raise the preview-policy question.
4. **Singapore:** the repo uses regular opening dates where LTA records earlier public previews; in particular CCL6 on 4 versus 12 July 2026. Early East–West naming and exact section kilometres need checking beyond a current network map.
5. **Shenzhen:** January 2025 SIAT/SUAT rename day and December 2025 Grand Theater passage day are not established. Simplified tram/Skyshuttle interchange markers deliberately compress real walking distances.
6. **Hangzhou:** October 2019 English Yuhang rename is month-only. Airport/railway-station continuous fares must not be mistaken for physically joined paid areas.
7. **Guangzhou/Foshan:** May/November 2006 and September 2021 naming rollouts lack exact days; historical tram previews require their timetable. Line 12 length definitions differ between sources.
8. **Chengdu:** the July 2023 English renaming batch, the Jincheng Plaza English forms and the 2020 Chengdu Academy of Governance rename lack exact days (latest attested days are used). Line 2's east section is recorded as cut back from 2025-12-20; its announced return on a new alignment on 2026-09-28 is not yet recorded. Line 8 phase 2, Line 10 phase 3 and several phase lengths are allocated from published totals.
9. **Across systems:** a published present-day total does not prove each historical `data-km` allocation, and a geographic SVG is not a survey. Original artwork provenance remains incompletely recovered for several older systems.

**Beijing:** exact days for Lixin/Gongzhufen, Bajiaocun/Bajiao Amusement Park, Xinhua Jie/Heping Men and Gucheng Lu/Gucheng renames remain unresolved. Those markers use current names; the map does not claim a complete day-resolved rename history. Historical Xi’erqi placement and section kilometres remain approximate. See the Beijing section for decisions and scope.

## Hong Kong — MTR / KCR (`hongkong`)

Assets: [events](../src/assets/hongkong/data/events.json), [line names](../src/assets/hongkong/data/lines.json), [SVG](../src/assets/hongkong/map.svg).

### Primary records and other focused references

- [KCRC: history][p-mtr-kcrc] — **New**. Operator chronology, KCR opening, 1913 Sheung Shui halt, electrification, Light Rail and later projects; distinguish ceremonies from first passenger operation.
- [KCR annual report 1921, paragraph 19][p-mtr-1921] — **Recovered**. Original government report, hosted by Histsyn; Hung Hom / Ho Man Tin halt evidence. Does not settle the repo's exact Hung Hom closure day.
- [KCR annual report 1935, paragraphs 59 and 102][p-mtr-1935] — **Recovered**. Che Kung Miu experimental halt, 16 November 1935; location at Bridge 12.
- [KCR annual report 1936, paragraph 41][p-mtr-1936] — **Recovered**. Che Kung Miu withdrawal, 1 October 1936.
- [KCR annual report 1976][p-mtr-1976] — **Recovered**. 1975 Kowloon terminal relocation and historical route context.
- [Hong Kong Railway Museum: Fanling–Sha Tau Kok Branch][p-mtr-stk-museum] — **Recovered**. Museum account and historical alignment/station illustrations; stronger provenance than a modern inferred route.
- [R. J. Phillips: Sha Tau Kok Branch, a study 1911–1928][p-mtr-stk-study] — **New**. Historical study, not an operator notice; consult its primary citations for partial opening and closure dates.
- [Contemporary Light Rail opening notice (newspaper scan)][p-mtr-lr-preview] — **Recovered**. 14–16 September 1988 preview versus 18 September regular service. Forum hosts the scan; the source is the newspaper notice.
- [Government daily information bulletin, 6 November 1991][p-mtr-lr-1991] — **Recovered**. Reports the extension opening on the preceding Sunday, 3 November; not 17 November.
- [LegCo: commissioning new railways, December 2003][p-mtr-lr-2003] — **Recovered**. Light Rail extensions opened 7 December; West Rail commissioning context.
- [MTR annual report 2018, Hong Kong transport operations][p-mtr-xrl-2018] — **Recovered**. Hong Kong high-speed rail opening, 23 September 2018.
- [MTR business overview][p-mtr-km] — **Recovered**. Published route lengths; Hong Kong XRL 25.7 km versus rounded 26 km project descriptions. Current totals do not certify every historical section allocation.
- [LegCo: XRL project report and alignment, December 2016][p-mtr-xrl-plan] — **Recovered**. Engineering alignment; Hong Kong section extent. The drawn continuation into Shenzhen is context, not additional Hong Kong kilometres.
- [MTR corporate profile][p-mtr-merger] — **New**. Rail merger on 2 December 2007; operator identity context for KCR/MTR labels.
- [MTR annual reports and results archive][p-mtr-reports] — **New**. Primary archive for modern openings, names and mileage. An archive index is a research route, not proof of an individual date.
- [MTR investor presentation, January 2018][p-mtr-island-west] — **New**. Slide 35: West Island Line 28 December 2014, Sai Ying Pun 29 March 2015, Kwun Tong extension 23 October 2016 and South Island Line 28 December 2016.
- [MTR PR030/22: East Rail cross-harbour opening][p-mtr-erl-2022] — **New**. Passenger service from 15 May 2022, Hung Hom–Admiralty and Exhibition Centre station.
- [EMSD: Tuen Ma Line Phase 1 opening][p-mtr-tml-2020] — **New**. 14 February 2020 and the distinction between Phase 1 and the later full line.

### SVG geography, identities and measurement limits

The SVG retains the title “MTR system map in English released to the public domain”, but its exact upstream Commons revision has not been recovered. Do not treat that embedded statement as full provenance for later additions. The background geography was replaced on 23 September 2026 using the sources below; the KCR/Sha Tau Kok reconstructions still require historical maps rather than modern track data.

- Coastlines and islands: © [OpenStreetMap contributors](https://www.openstreetmap.org/copyright), data under [ODbL 1.0](https://opendatacommons.org/licenses/odbl/1-0/), from [Geofabrik's Guangdong extract, including Hong Kong and Macau](https://download.geofabrik.de/asia/china/guangdong.html), snapshot `guangdong-260923.osm.pbf` (data through `2026-09-23T20:22:04Z`). Uses the mapped `natural=coastline` ways, including airport reclamation, harbour quays, Deep Bay and outlying islands; small offshore polygons below 0.5 SVG square units are omitted for legibility.
- Hong Kong/Shenzhen land colours: [Hong Kong administrative boundary, OSM relation 913110](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/913110). The visible border river uses the mapped water polygon [relation 9791660](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/9791660), rather than an invented gap between the land shapes.
- Registration: WGS84 coordinates fitted to the existing map using modern East Rail, Tsuen Wan, Tung Chung and Tuen Ma station control points. Coastlines were simplified with a topology-preserving tolerance of 0.25 SVG units, without moving railway paths or stations. This improves the background's geographic accuracy within the original map's approximate projection; it is not a survey. The background shows **present-day geography throughout playback**, not historical reclamation stages.

- Sha Tau Kok and early KCR: museum booklet and annual reports above. Historic curves and station positions remain approximate; a date citation does not certify their coordinates.
- Modern geographic cross-checks: [East Rail](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/4248589), [Tsuen Wan](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/269669), [Tung Chung](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/5317704), [Tuen Ma](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/6102298), [XRL](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/9405634).
- The XRL geometry was corrected through Kwai Chung/Shek Kong using mapped track plus the LegCo alignment. It continues into Shenzhen at the SVG boundary; only Hong Kong mileage is counted. Old Beacon Hill and Tsim Sha Tsui approaches are historical reconstructions, not current OSM railway.
- Light Rail is simplified. The documented allocation is 36.2 km today, with 4.4 km for 2003 extensions, 3.2 km for earlier Tin Shui Wai track and 28.6 km for the older Tuen Mun/Yuen Long area. Splits within that last group were prorated by drawn length: **estimates, not measured historical engineering lengths**.

<a id="shanghai"></a>

## Shanghai (`shanghai`)

Assets: [events](../src/assets/shanghai/data/events.json), [line names](../src/assets/shanghai/data/lines.json), [SVG](../src/assets/shanghai/map.svg).

### Primary records and other focused references

- [Timeline of Shanghai Metro][p-sh-timeline] — **Recovered**. User-specified completeness checklist; secondary, not final authority where dates conflict. Includes renames, transfers and temporary closures beyond this build-history scope.
- [Minhang government account: thirty years of Shanghai Metro][p-sh-early] — **New**. Line 1 historical opening stages; government-authored retrospective hosted by The Paper.
- [Shanghai government: South Railway Station and Xinzhuang opening][p-sh-xinzhuang] — **New**. Xinzhuang's first Jinshan Railway passenger train on 5 January 2025; do not confuse the mainline high-speed upgrade with a new metro line.
- [Shanghai government / Jinshan district: 1·5 timetable change][p-sh-jinshan-timetable] — **New**. Dated timetable and fare-map images for Jinshan services from 5 January 2025.
- [Shanghai Basic Facts 2024][p-sh-statistics] — **New**. Government network statistics and geographic context; aggregate length only, not a source for each SVG segment.
- [Shanghai Release: Jinghong Road temporary underground passage][p-sh-jinghong-open] — **New**. Operator announcement reproduced by Shanghai Release/Sina: 5 July 2025 opening and unchanged fare system.

### SVG geography, identities and measurement limits

Exact upstream SVG revision/author has not been recovered. Existing rivers and the original metro alignments are inherited illustration; the Shanghai timeline is a history source, not geographic evidence.

- [Songjiang Tram 1](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/14251681), [Tram 2](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/16159672) and [Line 9](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/214629) were recovered as alignment cross-checks. T2’s old Canghua spur uses the current T1 alignment as geometric context; historical service still needs the dated route description.
- Songjiang University Town’s short branch and local offsets at Sports Center depict simplified tram connections. They must not be read as survey-accurate co-location.
- Shanghai Railway Station, West Nanjing Road, Caoyang Road and other walking transfers need separate paid-area identities. A shared name or fare concession does not justify merging all markers or highlighting every line’s platforms.
- The early Jinjiang Park/Shanghai South and Zhangjiang High-Tech Park replacement alignments require historical maps. Modern line geometry cannot independently verify removed surface stations.

<a id="taipei"></a>

## Taipei (`taipei`)

Assets: [events](../src/assets/taipei/data/events.json), [line names](../src/assets/taipei/data/lines.json), [SVG](../src/assets/taipei/map.svg).

### Primary records and other focused references

- [Taipei Metro: Chronicles][p-tp-official] — **New**. Operator opening chronology and section lengths. Use the dated entries, not a later line name applied retrospectively.
- [Taipei Metro: 大事紀][p-tp-chronicles-zh] — **New**. Native-language operator chronology; cross-check opening/renaming wording against English translations.
- [Taipei Metro annual report 2015][p-tp-report-2015] — **New**. Operational route-name convention and Dingpu opening. Naming announcements and earlier through-service changes are not necessarily the same date.
- [Taipei Metro annual report 2007][p-tp-report-2007] — **New**. Historical sections and lengths; use to reconcile SVG kilometres instead of measuring the drawing.
- [Maokong Gondola: Chronicles][p-tp-gondola-history] — **Recovered**. Passenger opening 4 July 2007; later temporary suspensions are operational history, not demolition.
- [Maokong Gondola: system introduction][p-tp-gondola-profile] — **Recovered**. 4.03 km, four passenger stations and two angle stations. Only passenger stations contribute to the map count.
- [Maokong Gondola: transport connections][p-tp-gondola-access] — **Recovered**. Taipei Zoo MRT–gondola walking connection, approximately 350 m; not one paid-area station.

### Line and station inventory

Each history below includes a route/station table and references for intermediate station names and openings, supplementing the event prose. These are **secondary sources unless explicitly labelled as operator/government records above**. “SVG km now” is the current stored sum on the review date, shown for reconciliation—not a newly verified length. `0.0` on a predecessor means it has no active track today.

### SVG geography, identities and measurement limits

Exact upstream SVG revision/author has not been recovered. The pre-existing metro map provides the base geography; sources for openings are independent of that artwork.

- [Xiaobitan branch](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/4250381) and [Songshan–Xindian](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/4250357) underpin the branch correction: continue roughly south with the main line from Qizhang before bending west.
- [Maokong Gondola track way 71266575](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/71266575) and [route relation 17787820](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/17787820) supply the added cable alignment and four passenger stops. Intermediate angle facilities influence the route but are not passenger markers.
- The operator specifies a walk from Taipei Zoo MRT to the gondola. Banqiao/Circular Line and Xinpu/Xinpu Minsheng similarly need separate identities, not automatic grouping by proximity/name.
- Compare stored section lengths against operator historical tables: for example the operator chronology quotes 10.5 km for the initial Muzha section and 8.4 km for the Guting–Xindian extension, whereas existing allocations use different totals. These require reconciliation, not a blanket “verified” label.

## Singapore (`singapore`)

Assets: [events](../src/assets/singapore/data/events.json), [line names](../src/assets/singapore/data/lines.json), [SVG](../src/assets/singapore/map.svg).

### Primary records and other focused references

- [LTA: Rail Network][p-sg-network] — **New**. Official network map, line inventory, names and interchanges; current layout does not establish historic opening dates.
- [LTA: Downtown Line][p-sg-dtl] — **New**. Stages 1–3, Hume opening and published lengths; distinguish complete project lengths from operating sections.
- [LTA: Thomson-East Coast Line][p-sg-tel] — **New**. Opening stages, deferred stations and planned extensions; lists Stage 4 opening 23 June 2024.
- [LTA: Sengkang–Punggol LRT][p-sg-splrt] — **New**. Network layout and system-level opening years, not every later infill station day.
- [SBS Transit: Ridership][p-sg-sbs] — **New**. Operator states Sengkang LRT opened 18 January 2003 and Punggol LRT 29 January 2005.
- [National Library Board: Woodlands MRT station][p-sg-woodlands] — **New**. Historical account with primary newspaper and government references for the Woodlands extension.
- [SMRT operations review 2020: milestones][p-sg-smrt] — **New**. Operator historical milestones; useful corroboration of early network stages, not every exact date.
- [LTA: Punggol Coast to open for passenger service][p-sg-punggol] — **New**. 10 December 2024 opening and North East Line extension.
- [LTA: Circle Line 6][p-sg-ccl6] — **New**. Three new stations, closed loop, passenger opening 12 July 2026; links the separate 4 July preview notice.
- [LTA newsroom: Circle Line 6 notices][p-sg-ccl6-preview] — **New**. 14 May 2026 notice explicitly distinguishes 4 July public preview from 12 July passenger service; exact article should replace this index link when available.
- [LTA annual report 2024/25][p-sg-report-2025] — **New**. Recent network milestones, including Teck Lee LRT opening; simplified LRT markers intentionally omit most intermediate stops.

### SVG geography, identities and measurement limits

Exact upstream SVG revision/author has not been recovered. LTA’s official network maps substantiate station names and connectivity, but they are schematic and do not independently certify geographic Bézier coordinates. A complete geographic retrace of the inherited Singapore SVG has **not** been established by the recovered research.

- Use LTA’s line pages for operating length and stage maps, plus the station tables below for opening chronology. Do not count planned TEL stations merely because they appear on a project map.
- Bukit Panjang, Sengkang and Punggol LRT use simplified track coverage. Intermediate stop openings are not all separate markers/events; this is a scope choice, not evidence those stops never existed.
- Document line-total rounding and branch accounting before treating section `data-km` as precise engineering measurements. The completed Circle Line’s ring and Marina Bay spur need consistent treatment.

### SVG geography, identities and measurement limits

Recovered original artwork: [Tokyo Subway Linemap en.svg](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Tokyo_Subway_Linemap_en.svg), Yveltal, CC BY-SA 4.0; the source used was the June 2020 map. Keep its file-history attribution rather than substituting a similarly named Tokyo map. Only Tokyo Metro/Toei and their predecessors are in scope; faded private/JR connecting railways in the supplied map were excluded.

- Recovered alignment comparisons: [Ginza](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/8026074), [Yurakucho](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/443269), [Fukutoshin](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/5375678), [Hanzomon](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/443279), [Namboku](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/7730199), [Mita](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/443286), [Oedo](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/3355612).
- Smoothing and shorter interchange symbols intentionally move local control points/markers slightly. These are cartographic edits, not track relocation events. Modern OSM cannot prove historic Manseibashi, Shimbashi, Omotesando or pre-2020 Shibuya platform coordinates.
- Use the operator’s historical-platform and Shibuya-relocation plans above, plus the station-specific histories below, to distinguish these real relocations from drawing adjustments.
- Namboku/Mita shared track and Marunouchi/Ogikubo predecessor accounting need line-specific length definitions; summing advertised line lengths can double-count physical track.

## Shenzhen (`shenzhen`)

Assets: [events](../src/assets/shenzhen/data/events.json), [line names](../src/assets/shenzhen/data/lines.json), [SVG](../src/assets/shenzhen/map.svg).

### Primary records and other focused references

- [Shenzhen Metro construction group: development overview][p-sz-overview] — **New**. Operator retrospective for early openings and phases. Future-tense projects on an old page are not opening confirmation.
- [Shenzhen Transport Bureau: Line 13 northern extension opens][p-sz-13north] — **New**. 28 June 2026, Shangwu–Lisonglang, 11 stations and 19.25 km (19.3 when rounded).
- [Pingshan government release: Shenzhen's first Skyshuttle opens][p-sz-sky-open] — **New**. 28 December 2022, 8.5 km, eleven stops and metro/mainline connections. Simplified map does not draw all eleven.
- [BYD: Pingshan Skyshuttle opening][p-sz-sky-builder] — **New**. Builder confirmation of opening and route/connection concept; references to future connecting lines are not current interchanges.
- [Shenzhen planning bureau: Line 13 engineering alignment][p-sz-13-plan] — **Recovered**. Engineering geometry used alongside OSM; approved plan is not independently proof of opening date.

### SVG geography, identities and measurement limits

Recovered original artwork: [Shenzhen Metro (Rapid Transit) System Map.svg](<https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Shenzhen_Metro_(Rapid_Transit)_System_Map.svg>), Wahsaw, CC BY-SA 4.0. Its revision predates some additions. Geography for later lines was supplemented with mapped track and station coordinates, not inferred solely from a current schematic.

- Added/revised alignments: [Line 3](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/2287559), [Line 6 Branch](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/14953716), [Line 7](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/6781618), [Line 8](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/13176926), [Line 12](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/14944728), [Line 13](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/18470804), [Line 16](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/15059917).
- Additional cross-checks: [Line 5](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/2752389), [Line 11](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/9318441). Small offsets remain around separate paid-area markers; a smooth curve is not a surveyed centreline.
- Longhua Tram and Pingshan Skyshuttle are simplified. The Skyshuttle drawing was deliberately adjusted to reach the selected metro interchange markers, like the existing Guanlan representation. This is **not evidence that the actual tracks pass through those metro platforms**. Real walking/paid-area arrangements must be taken from station plans.
- Use the Line 13 engineering report above for an independent alignment cross-check; future planned connections shown there are not historical service.

## Hangzhou (`hangzhou`)

Assets: [events](../src/assets/hangzhou/data/events.json), [line names](../src/assets/hangzhou/data/lines.json), [SVG](../src/assets/hangzhou/map.svg).

### Primary records and other focused references

- [Hangzhou news: Line 1 public rides, 19 November 2012][p-hz-preview1] — **New**. Reports public rides beginning 18 November; explains why the repo predates the usual 24 November opening date.
- [Central government: Hangzhou Metro opens, 24 November 2012][p-hz-regular1] — **New**. Regular opening date, distinct from the public preview used by this project.
- [Hangzhou news: Line 2 trial rides, 17 November 2014][p-hz-preview2] — **New**. Public preview 18–22 November; regular service 24 November.
- [Hangzhou news: East Railway Station transfer guide][p-hz-east-square] — **New**. 6 November 2021 Line 6 opening and separate East Square paid area.
- [Hangzhou Metro release: Shentangqiao passage opens tomorrow][p-hz-shentang-open] — **New**. Operator-authored notice reproduced by Hangzhou news: paid-area passage opens 1 November 2022.
- [Hangzhou government: 2021 station-name approval][p-hz-name-approval] — **New**. Approval of names; does not alone establish the later 26 January 2022 passenger-signage change.
- [Government account: Zhijiang Culture Center station rename][p-hz-zhijiang-name] — **New**. Effective 1 February 2024, formerly Zhipu Road.
- [Hangzhou news: Jingchang Road opening][p-hz-jingchang] — **New**. Announces 30 June 2025 infill opening.
- [Hangzhou environmental authority: airport express engineering report][p-hz-19-plan] — **Recovered**. Route and station geography for Line 19; distinguish approved alignment from operating history.
- [Zhejiang Online / Qianjiang Evening News: two station renames][p-hz-renames-2022] — **New**. Reports operator confirmation: Dongxinyuan and Linpingnan names take effect on 26 January 2022.
- [China Association of Metros: 2025 operating-line statistics][p-gz-camet] also covers the 2025 Hangzhou Line 5 extension.

### SVG geography, identities and measurement limits

The supplied Hangzhou SVG is the geographic base, but its exact upstream artwork URL/revision has not been recovered. Do not attribute it to an unrelated Commons schematic. Later additions were checked against station coordinates and mapped track, supplemented by the airport express engineering report.

- Reconstructed/corrected corridors: [Line 3 main](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/13538220), [Shima branch](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/14280625), [Line 5](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/10386952), [Line 7](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/13061278), [Line 9](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/13060896), [Line 19](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/14613131).
- [Line 1](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/4627560) and [Line 10](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/13535687) were comparison alignments. The later audit corrected Gudang Xincun, Yicheng Road and Pinglan Road positions while retaining compact interchange offsets.
- East Railway Station and East Square remain separate paid areas even after eligible tickets gain continuous fares. Shentangqiao’s actual passage opening has its own 1 November 2022 source.
- Unopened Tiaoxi and deferred intermediate platforms must not be inferred open from the full planned project length. Verify operating extent before using a line-total kilometre figure.

## Guangzhou / Foshan (`guangfo`)

Assets: [events](../src/assets/guangfo/data/events.json), [line names](../src/assets/guangfo/data/lines.json), [SVG](../src/assets/guangfo/map.svg).

### Primary records and other focused references

- [China Association of Metros: 2025 operating-line statistics][p-gz-camet] — **New**. Industry statistical table: opening dates, operating lengths and station counts, including GZ 10/12 and Huangpu Tram 2, and Hangzhou Line 5 extension.
- [Southcn: Chigang Line 12 opening][p-gz-chigang] — **New**. 13 February 2026 opening of previously bypassed Line 12 platforms.
- [Southcn: Foshan Line 3 through operation][p-gz-f3-through] — **New**. Reports Foshan rail authority approval: 18 June 2026 full connection. Need separate evidence for deferred Foshan Railway Station.
- [China News Service: Foshan hub opening][p-gz-foshan-station] — **New**. 1 July 2026 railway/metro station opening, distinct from 18 June through running.
- [Guangdong Transport Department: Nanhai Tram extension][p-gz-nanhai-open] — **New**. 29 November 2022 extension; retrospectively confirms 18 August 2021 initial opening; Linyuexi same-platform transfer without leaving the paid area.
- [Guangzhou government: South Railway Station paid-area passage][p-gz-south-transfer] — **New**. 21 January 2024, linking Foshan Line 2 to Guangzhou Lines 2, 7 and 22.

### SVG geography, identities and measurement limits

Recovered base artwork: [Guangzhou Metro Map en.svg](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Guangzhou_Metro_Map_en.svg), ASDFGH, CC BY-SA 3.0. The user-supplied `og.svg` preserved Foshan geometry; `ref.svg` was a later network cross-check, not a replacement for geographic alignment. Those input filenames alone are not durable external citations.

- Modern additions/corrections: [Line 3](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/9841062), [5](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/7483883), [7](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/7520751), [10](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/19283541), [11](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/18132492), [12 east](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/19283546), [12 west](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/19283549), [13](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/6728313), [14](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/8338723), [22](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/13987999), [Huangpu Tram 2](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/19382702).
- Restored/extended Foshan corridors: [Line 2](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/13604443), [Line 3](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/15054264), [Nanhai Tram](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/13103097). Use dated notices for which part of these modern routes was open at each event.
- Ersha Island and other added stations were recalibrated against geographic coordinates. Chinese map coordinates may be GCJ-02 while OSM is WGS84; mixing them shifts stations systematically. Bézier smoothing and compact interchange offsets remain presentation adjustments.
- Western canvas/rivers were extended using mapped water geometry to avoid clipping inherited rivers. That edit is a present-day background repair, not a history of land reclamation or river changes.
- Nanhai’s Linyuexi paid-area transfer is explicitly supported by the Guangdong Transport Department notice. APM/metro and several tram walking links must not be assumed to share paid areas merely because a passage exists.

<a id="chengdu"></a>

## Chengdu (`chengdu`)

Assets: [events](../src/assets/chengdu/data/events.json), [line names](../src/assets/chengdu/data/lines.json), [SVG](../src/assets/chengdu/map.svg).

### Primary records and other focused references

- [Chengdu Metro app API: station list][p-cd-chengdu-metro-app-api-station-list] — **New**. Every line, official English station name and transfer line; the live copy of 2026-09-24 was compared with an archived copy of 2024-07-10. The transfer lists are the test for designated interchanges (§6).
- [Chengdu Rail Transit: English station list (archived 2017–2023)][p-cd-chengdu-rail-transit-english-station-lis] — **New**. Pre-2023 English station names; more than 70 captures from 2017-12-08 to 2023-01-05.
- [Official network map, February 2023 (archived)][p-cd-official-network-map-february-2023-archi] — **New**. Pre-revision English names; the December 2024 and December 2025 editions show the post-July-2023 names, Line S3 and Tram Line 2.
- [Sichuan Online, 2025-09-16][p-cd-sichuan-online-2025-09-16] and [China News Service, 2025-12-05][p-cd-china-news-service-2025-12-05] — **New**. Operator figures for Line 17 phase 2, Line 13 and Line 30 (lengths, stations, first public day).
- [Red Star News via Sina, 2026-09-24][p-cd-red-star-news-via-sina-2026-09-24] — **New**. Announces the Line 2 east section's return on 2026-09-28 on its new alignment (after this register's date; see the SVG notes).
- [Wikimedia Commons: Chengdu Rail Transit icon][p-cd-wikimedia-commons-chengdu-rail-transit-i] — **New**. Source of the logo; public domain as a text logo, with a trademark notice. The app preserves the complete mark’s shapes and proportions, with its original blue fills and no added background.
- Each event row below cites the notices and reports behind that date; station lists and English names otherwise follow the operator app and the archived English station list.

### SVG geography, identities and measurement limits

- **Artwork.** The supplied SVG is an Illustrator export of a geographic Chengdu Metro map (roughly the mid-2024 network: it has Qingdao Road but not Line 27 or the Line 8 phase 2 extensions). Its upstream source and licence are not recorded. Lines are outlined strokes; their centrelines were recovered exactly from the two sides of each ribbon (§12). Stripe-drawn shared stretches (Lines 5/6 at Xibei Bridge, 18/19 to the airport) were rebuilt as full-width parallels, and Line 1’s doubly drawn trunk at Sihe as one track with the Wugensong branch leaving it.
- **Added lines.** Lines 13, 27, 30 and S3 and the extensions of Lines 8, 10 and 17 follow OpenStreetMap route relations ([OSM: Line 13][cd-osm-line-13], [OSM: Line 27][cd-osm-line-27], [OSM: Line 30][cd-osm-line-30], [OSM: Line S3][cd-osm-line-s3], [OSM: Line 8][cd-osm-line-8], [OSM: Line 10][cd-osm-line-10], [OSM: Line 17][cd-osm-line-17]), retrieved 2026-09-24. Coordinates were fitted to the artwork with an affine transform on 20 line termini, then a Gaussian local correction from 329 matched stations (median residual 1.1 units, about 20 m).
- **Water and outside land.** Water is the union of OpenStreetMap `natural=water`, `waterway=riverbank` and `landuse=reservoir` areas retrieved 2026-09-24, through the same fit; ponds under 60 square units are dropped and outlines simplified at 0.4 units. Land outside [OSM: Chengdu municipality boundary][cd-osm-chengdu-municipality-boundary] is tinted. The background is present-day geography throughout playback.
- **Western extent.** The frame extends 1,552.494 units westward to put Tianfu Square at its horizontal midpoint. All original coordinates shift right by that amount; track shapes, station alignment and scale are unchanged. The added western water areas use the same geographic fit and 2026-09-24 OpenStreetMap data, with the municipality boundary extended across the new frame.
- **Placement exceptions.** Line 10’s Terminal 2 of Shuangliu International Airport is moved 14 units along its track so the out-of-station pair with East of Terminal 2 reads as two stations (2.4 r). Line 30’s Financial City North is moved 4.5 units clear of Line 18. At the airport, Lines 10 and 19 run in parallel closer than the marker clearance (tolerated shared corridor, §5).
- **Tram.** Chengdu Tram Line 2 is drawn as simplified track; its metro interchanges shown in `data-lines` are the same-named Chengdu West Railway Station (from 2019-12-27), Tianhe Road (2019-12-27) and Wangcong Temple (2020-12-18, when Line 6 opened). The official map also marks Xipu Campus ↔ Xingye North Street and Longyin ↔ Zitonggong; they are not included (different names, no dated designation).
- **Kilometres.** Published line totals (China Association of Metros 2024 and operator releases) govern: Line 18’s 69.39 km is split by drawn length because its published 55.95 km first section conflicts with station spacing (≈48.2 km); Line 3’s phases 2 and 3 and Line 4’s 2017 extensions share their published remainder by drawn length; Line 19 counts the shared Line 18 track from Tianfu Station at Line 18’s own allocation (total 99.7 km; the operator says over 100 km).
- **Colours.** The artwork’s colours are Pantone values (Line 1 Blue 072 C, Line 2 171 C, Line 7 310 C, Line 10 293 C, Line 17 352 C, Line 18 3165 C, Line 19 2718 C) and are kept; Lines 13 and 27 use OpenStreetMap’s values in the same family (103 C, 299 C). The operator app uses a separate screen palette.

<a id="beijing"></a>

## Beijing (`beijing`)

New map and research, **24 September 2026**. Public passenger history begins on **15 January 1971**; the 1969 inauguration and restricted military tracks are not public openings. Includes the subway, Capital and Daxing airport railways, S1, and Xijiao / Yizhuang T1 as simplified tracks. The last recorded change is **30 June 2026** (Tongyun Men); the present-day review also includes the May 2026 Pingguoyuan interchange. Temporary suspensions do not erase built infrastructure. National/suburban rail, construction projects and never-opened platforms are outside scope.

### Primary records and focused references

- **Early public openings:** [Beijing Transport Vocational College’s historical account](https://www.bjjt.edu.cn/info/2005/19929.htm) records 1971-01-15, **1971-08-15**, 1971-11-07 and 1973-04-23. August 15 takes precedence over August 5 in the English summary table. The [municipal history](https://www.beijing.gov.cn/gate/big5/www.beijing.gov.cn/ywdt/gzdt/202410/t20241005_3911383.html) corroborates the 1987 loop reorganization.
- **Line 13 eastern opening:** the [transport commission](https://jtw.beijing.gov.cn/xxgk/xwfbh/201912/t20191209_1007437.html) gives **2003-01-28**, correcting January 9 in some station lists. [Beijing’s construction gazette](https://zjw.beijing.gov.cn/bjjs/gcjs/sznj/zjcg/sz/10934391/2021020215473385426.pdf) covers the 1991–2010 projects.
- **Olympic Sports Center:** [the October 10 contemporary report](https://travel.sina.com.cn/around/2008-10-10/114429534.shtml) says the station first opened on **October 9**, rather than October 10 in the summary chronology. The Olympic branch’s July 19, 2008 opening initially served eligible Olympic passengers; this counts as public passenger operation.
- **Xi’erqi relocation:** [December 25, 2010 contemporary report](https://news.sina.com.cn/c/2010-12-25/125121705654.shtml). The new building was north of the old one; reported building separation is not a platform-centre coordinate. The old marker is approximately 260 m south on the mapped railway, not a surveyed historical platform point.
- **Qinghe reroute:** [transport commission construction notice](https://jtw.beijing.gov.cn/xxgk/xwfbh/201912/t20191209_1007592.html) and [February 17 contemporary report](https://news.sina.cn/gn/2019-02-17/detail-ihrfqzka6456088.d.html). The new alignment carried trains from **2019-02-17**; the station itself opened **2019-12-30**, not December 28. Track and station dates are deliberately separate.
- **Fares/transfers:** [2007 unified-fare report](https://news.sina.com.cn/c/2007-10-10/161514056608.shtml), [Dongzhimen’s original 2003 transfer arrangement](https://news.sina.com.cn/c/2003-01-23/0334884481.shtml), [Xizhimen’s transfer history](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/西直门站), and the [operator’s current ticket rules](https://www.mtr.bj.cn/page/instructions.html). Airport railways retain separate fares and operators, with internal station-complex capsules under the spec’s cross-system rule. Muxidi and Dazhongsi remain separate stations with connectors.
- **Muxidi:** [municipal transfer explanation](https://www.beijing.gov.cn/fuwu/bmfw/sy/jrts/202401/t20240117_3537679.html); a promised future paid-area corridor is not an opened corridor. [Dazhongsi’s station history](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/大钟寺站) describes the separate Line 12 / 13 stations.
- **2024 openings:** [municipal opening report](https://www.beijing.gov.cn/ywdt/gzdt/202412/t20241215_3965616.html), covering Lines 3/12 and Changping’s Jimen Qiao extension / Zhufang Bei.
- **2025 delayed stations and extensions:** [Dahong Men, November 8](https://www.beijing.gov.cn/fuwu/bmfw/sy/jrts/202511/t20251109_4266635.html); [MTR’s Line 17 middle-section opening release](https://www.mtr.com.hk/archive/corporate/en/press_release/PR-25-094-E.pdf); [Beijing Subway notices](https://www.bjsubway.com/) for Line 18 and Line 6’s Luyang extension on December 27; [the national urban-rail association’s 2025 new-section inventory](https://infosharingp2-oss.camet.org.cn/resources/manual/2026/01/04/759313381470277.pdf).
- **2026:** [Wangjing Xi on Line 17, January 31](https://english.beijing.gov.cn/livinginbeijing/transportation/beijingsubway/202601/t20260131_4480193.html); [Pingguoyuan’s three-line interchange, May 16](https://www.beijing.gov.cn/fuwu/bmfw/sy/jrts/202605/t20260516_4653696.html); [Tongyun Men, June 30](https://www.beijing.gov.cn/fuwu/bmfw/sy/jrts/202606/t20260629_4737846.html), [confirmed after opening](https://www.beijing.gov.cn/fuwu/bmfw/sy/jrts/202607/t20260701_4741334.html). A closure-only filter would incorrectly discard the new Pingguoyuan transfer: the old Line 1 marker stays drawn, separate from the 6/S1 capsule until May 16.
- **Not open as of the review:** [Fushouling exit-design procurement, September 24](https://ggzyfw.beijing.gov.cn/jyxxggjtbyqs/20260924/5726978.html), [Guangqumenwai Line 17 construction permit](https://ghzrzyw.beijing.gov.cn/zhengwuxinxi/tzgg/dc/202606/t20260623_4712158.html). No speculative Line 22, M101, Line 13 split, Line 1 branch or Daxing Airport Express northern extension is drawn. Gaojiayuan, Hongmiao and Taoranqiao on Line 14 also remain unopened.

### Event-by-event source index

Descriptions are the map’s event inventory. The line histories above provide the baseline references; the focused records above settle the early-service, 2003, 2008, relocation, transfer and recent-opening corrections. Routine station upgrades, entrances, rolling stock, fares without a transfer-topology change, and temporary closures are not extra build events.

### Names: decisions and remaining evidence gaps

- The 1984 northern section is labelled **Second Phase**, then **Loop Line** from 1987-12-28, then **Line 2** from 2002-07-24, following the [Chinese system chronology](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/北京地铁). The last exact day is a secondary historical reference, not a newly recovered primary naming notice. “Line 1” is used as the English name of the original first-phase service; the later Chinese change from 一线 to 1号线 does not create a different English name.
- **Fuba Line → Line 1**, 2000-06-28; **Airport Express → Capital Airport Express**, 2019-09-24 ([contemporary report](https://ie.bjd.com.cn/a/201909/24/AP5d89ca83e4b021e796143804.html)); **Line 1 / Batong**, 2021-08-29, models the merged service and permanent retirement of the redundant Sihui/Batong and Sihui Dong/Line 1 platforms. It is a combined service label, not a claim that the two infrastructure lines ceased to have administrative identities.
- **Broadcasting Institute → Communication University of China**, 2007-04-11, follows the April 12 contemporary report cited by [the station history](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/传媒大学站) ([archived report](https://web.archive.org/web/20171205143554/http://news.163.com/07/0412/18/3BT8B3GM000120GU.html)). The university’s 2004 rename was not the station rename.
- **City Railway / Line 13:** the numerical designation was already used at opening ([contemporary 2002 report](https://news.sohu.com/22/35/news203453522.shtml)); a [2025 retrospective](https://xinwen.bjd.com.cn/content/s6940aa98e4b06b6f7a7f8649.html) instead describes 2003-12-31 as formal naming. The map uses Line 13 from its first public day, without presenting the retrospective as a definite rename. **Olympic Branch / Line 8** likewise coexisted as names; it is not given a fabricated rename date.
- **Unresolved historical names:** Lixin → Gongzhufen (1980s), Bajiaocun → Bajiao Amusement Park (1990s), Xinhua Jie → Heping Men (1987), and Gucheng Lu → Gucheng (2008) lack verified exact days in the retrieved evidence. Markers retain current names; the opening event identifies Lixin explicitly. [Beijing Daily’s archive investigation](https://news.bjd.com.cn/2025/09/24/11321556.shtml) specifically contradicts treating the amusement park’s 1986 opening as the station rename. [The January 31, 2008 Gucheng reconstruction report](https://news.sina.com.cn/c/2008-01-31/021913356273s.shtml) still uses Gucheng Lu; the February 1 facility opening is not evidence of an exact rename day. Do not “complete” these by inventing January 1 dates.
- English names use consistent current translations across a physical station. Beijing’s 2020–24 English-signage revisions were gradual and differed by operator/station; they are not modelled as simultaneous station renames. Qinghe’s added Chinese 站 is not a new English station identity. The proposed 2026 Tongzhou Beiguan rename is not recorded as an implemented change.

### SVG geography, identities and measurement limits

- **No starting map artwork:** railway ways, stop positions, Beijing’s municipal boundary ([OSM relation 912940](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/912940)) and water geometry were retrieved on **2026-09-24** from [OpenStreetMap](https://www.openstreetmap.org/), © OpenStreetMap contributors, [ODbL 1.0](https://www.openstreetmap.org/copyright). The SVG and preview are derived, edited geographic illustrations. The logo is [AlgaeGraphix’s public-domain geometric artwork](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Beijing_Subway_simplified_logo.svg), with JaJaWa’s official-colour revision.
- Coordinates use **WGS84 / UTM zone 50N (EPSG:32650)**, then `x = (easting − 408000) / 20`, `y = (4466000 − northing) / 20`, in a `4400 × 5300` viewBox. This places 1 km at 50 SVG units. Track, water and land use the same projection. The [Amap current network](https://map.amap.com/service/subway?srhdata=1100_drw_beijing.json) cross-checks current stops and English spellings; Zhufang Bei’s missing OSM stop position uses Amap coordinates with **GCJ-02 converted to WGS84**, projected onto the mapped railway.
- Curves follow rail-way vertices, simplified at 9 m and interpolated with cubic Béziers. Local station-scale offsets keep interchange lines legible; they are cartographic adjustments, not engineering survey geometry. Contiguous opening spans remain single paths, split only for actual historical changes, reassignments or reroutes. No per-station track fragmentation, station text, legend artwork or background labels are embedded.
- The original Line 1 connector uses [OSM way 114647613](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/114647613). The pre-2021 parallel Sihui–Sihui Dong alignment uses mapped railway ways, with exaggerated platform separation for legibility. Qinghe’s old alignment uses **2018-01-01 OSM history**, replaced on 2019-02-17. Xi’erqi’s old marker is approximate, as noted above. Older small track shifts and rebuilt platform footprints without recovered engineering drawings remain generalized; present-day geometry is not proof that every metre was unchanged since opening.
- Capital Airport Express includes both the outbound route and the return branch from [relation 2062999](https://www.openstreetmap.org/relation/2062999). Its reversing Terminal 3 spur is traversed twice by the continuous SVG path, but physical route kilometres are counted once. It is not an invented triangle between airport station points.
- The geography is **present-day throughout playback**. Beijing land is the normal land colour; neighbouring Hebei is the foreign-land colour, including the Daxing Airport approach. Lakes and mapped riverbank polygons preserve holes; river/canal centreline buffers fill gaps where OSM has no bank polygon. Those small-watercourse widths are cartographic estimates, not historical or surveyed banks. Land and water simplification tolerances are 12 m and 7 m respectively.
- Xizhimen, Dongzhimen, Sihui and Sihui Dong retain their separate-fare/out-of-station phases. Muxidi and Dazhongsi have distinct station IDs; circle-to-capsule replacements share an identity with dated `--YYYY-MM-DD` versions. Pingguoyuan’s former Line 1 station and newer 6/S1 complex are separate until the 2026 internal connection. Xijiao/T1 have tracks only except memberships at Bagou/Rongchang Dongjie, following the simplified-service rule.
- **Kilometres are approximate by opening stage.** Modern totals use the published line overviews, checked against [the operator’s interstation-distance tables](https://www.mtr.bj.cn/service/line/distable/line-1.html). These definitions differ: construction length, running length and station-centre distance can include different turnbacks/overruns. Values are allocated and rounded to one decimal per historical span; they are not SVG arc lengths. The sum currently shown is **899.6 km**, not the municipal **909 km** headline. That definition/allocation discrepancy remains unresolved; the map does not force an arbitrary extra 9.4 km into a section. Tram stops are deliberately excluded from the station statistic, and Line 1/Batong is one current legend entry, so app counts must not be equated with the official “30 lines / 541 stations” per-line inventory.

## Geography and artwork: shared limitations

[OpenStreetMap contributors](https://www.openstreetmap.org/copyright) provide the modern mapped alignments linked above. The listed object IDs were recovered from the earlier geographic research; their live versions may change. Use the object history or a dated snapshot when reproducing an edit. These are crowdsourced spatial checks, not railway engineering certification or proof of historical opening dates.

The original supplied SVG and later adjustments are separate layers of provenance. A Commons licence applies to its identified artwork, not automatically to every later background, logo or geometry addition. Follow the linked file pages for the full attribution/file history. Exact original source revisions remain an explicit gap where they could not be recovered; a visually similar map is not sufficient attribution.

The maps deliberately simplify curves, parallel tracks and interchange capsules. `data-station` IDs are repository identifiers, not official station codes. Colours may be inherited or adapted. Neither identifier spelling, symbol shape nor a tiny local display offset needs a historical event, but the station identity and line membership they represent do.

### Length reconciliation still required

- The line tables show **stored** active kilometre sums, not a claim that every figure was obtained directly from a primary source.
- Check each opening section against its published _operating_ length; project length can include unopened stations, sidings, depots or future extensions.
- Preserve the source precision before rounding to the SVG’s one decimal. When a sourced total is split between paths solely for animation/relocation/line reassignment, record the allocation method rather than presenting each split as independently measured.
- Historical KCR/Light Rail estimates, Tokyo shared corridors, Taipei differing historical section figures and Guangzhou Line 12’s 27.9/28.8 km discrepancy particularly need this treatment. A matching modern total cannot validate all earlier totals.
- Station counts are derived from the spec’s identities and simplification policy. They will not always equal an operator’s published “stations”, which may count interchange platforms once per line or include all intermediate LRT/tram stops.

## Updating the register

For a new or corrected fact, update the relevant event row **and** the applicable line/station/geometry entry. Record the actual passenger date, source publication date, exact article/PDF page or chronology entry, and what the source establishes. Prefer a stable article URL over a homepage/search result. Preserve an archive URL for an inaccessible original, and label a reported/approximate date honestly.

Do not promote a recovered citation to “verified” without reading the relevant passage. Resolve mixed rows description by description; e.g. a line-opening announcement may substantiate the route but say nothing about the English name rollout or whether a transfer is within the paid area. Check SVG/legend boundaries against the revised event date after a data correction.

This register is documentation only; it is not imported by the application. It does not add sources/network/audit JSON to the runtime data model.

## Link definitions

Reference-style links keep repeated event rows readable and keep each source URL in one place.

## Nanjing

Checked through **2026-09-24**. The map includes the fifteen operating metro/suburban lines
(1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 10, S1, S2, S3, S4, S6, S7, S8, S9), plus Hexi and Qilin trams as
simplified tracks. S4 includes its operating Chuzhou section; the unbuilt connection into
Nanjing and its reserved stations are not shown. Lines 6, 9, 11 and the other announced
extensions are not treated as open. The [September 23 operator service announcement](https://www.nanjing.gov.cn/zzb/hdhy/hygq_72666/202609/t20260923_5916215.html)
and [April S2 opening report](https://www.jiangning.gov.cn/sjb/xwzx/gzdt/202604/t20260424_5829714.html)
were checked alongside the individual line histories. This is a construction history:
COVID, flooding, repairs, service hours and temporary testing suspensions do not remove
unchanged infrastructure.

### Opening and conversion chronology

The English and Chinese [system history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing_Metro),
[station inventory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Nanjing_Metro_stations) and individual
line articles were used as indexes, then compared with contemporary reports. In particular,
September 3, 2005 was the inauguration, not the first public passenger service.

### Geometry, identities and distance

- Drawn from railway ways and stop positions in [OpenStreetMap](https://www.openstreetmap.org/),
  © OpenStreetMap contributors, [ODbL 1.0](https://www.openstreetmap.org/copyright), retrieved
  September 24, 2026. All railways and geography share EPSG:32650, at 50 SVG units/km.
- Current route relations: 1533791, 3413088, 7885542, 9588280, 17417176, 15336345,
  1529760, 7913265, 19979191, 7913253, 15804056, 13200480, 9584584, 9588173,
  9584574; tram routes 16159337 and 16159339. These supply alignment, not opening dates.
- The retired Andemen approach is recovered from pre-conversion relation 1529760/version 19;
  the old Hexi southern alignment from way 736715174/version 9. Recovering deleted OSM
  nodes is a cartographic reconstruction, not evidence of service dates. The reusable track
  north/west of those changes is not duplicated into one path per station interval.
- Background land is present-day Jiangsu, relation 913012; Anhui has the foreign-land fill.
  Yangtze bank polygons preserve islands; Xuanwu, Mochou, Shijiu, Gucheng and Jinniu lakes
  use mapped polygons. Historical shoreline changes are not animated. Minor ponds and streams
  are omitted. Curves simplify mapped vertices, and local station offsets keep capsules straight
  and nearby non-serving lines clear; they are not survey drawings.
- Marker versions share a stable identity through circle-to-capsule upgrades. Zhushanlu is the
  explicit out-of-station exception. Tram stops are omitted apart from their existing heavy-rail
  interchange markers, per the shared specification.
- The current inventory has **293 line/platform memberships at 260 distinct marker identities**.
  Official headline station totals often count a transfer once for each line: the December 2025
  announcement's 267 equals the sum of platform counts before adding S2's sixteen and S4's ten.
- `data-km` uses published route/section lengths rounded to tenths; the early Line 1 sightseeing
  and reused western sections are apportioned within the published line totals. These subdivisions
  are approximate, not surveyed distances. Current metro/suburban paths sum to **624.5 km**;
  including the simplified trams gives **641.3 km**. This does not exactly reproduce the roughly
  625.7 km headline metro total: published construction and operating lengths differ. No invisible
  length or phantom track was added to force a match.
- [Nanjing Metro logo](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Nanjing_Metro_Logo.svg): public-domain
  geometric mark; the Commons SVG was corrected by Tomchen1989 using the operator's mark.

### Remaining evidence gaps

These are limitations, not verified exact-date claims:

1. **Early sightseeing extensions:** contemporary reports establish the May 15 Xinjiekou–Xiaohang
   service and August 12 all-station service. Earlier plans mentioned June trips to Gulou and July
   trips to Maigaoqiao, but an exact implemented start date for each was not established. A later
   personal account describes a June 4 Gulou trip. The map does not invent June 1 / July 1 dates;
   north-of-Xinjiekou track therefore currently starts at the verified August 12 date. The May 12
   invited press/newspaper-reader rehearsal is not treated as regular public sightseeing service.
2. **Linjiang / YOGSP:** a secondary station account places the addition of “YOGSP” in December
   2016, without a verified day. The stable base name **Linjiang** is retained across the timeline;
   the full later suffix and its transition remain unencoded. It is not backdated to opening.
3. **Caochangmen and Xuzhuang subtitles:** Chinese institutional prefixes changed to subtitles in 2018. The cited approval is February 13; reports describe implementation in March. The map uses
   the English base names throughout, rather than claiming a synchronized English signage change
   on the approval date. Other inconsistent English abbreviations in the network (CPU, NJCI,
   NMU / JIETT) likewise do not establish dated operational renames.

Validation checks SVG/legend/event boundary agreement, station memberships, unique IDs, path counts
and distances. Browser checks exercise all event dates and adjacent days in both directions,
search identities, highlighted-line filtering, tooltip dismissal, mobile resize and the home preview.
These checks validate the implementation; they do not resolve the historical evidence gaps above.

<a id="chongqing"></a>

## Chongqing — Rail Transit (`chongqing`)

Assets: [events](../src/assets/chongqing/data/events.json), [line names](../src/assets/chongqing/data/lines.json), [SVG](../src/assets/chongqing/map.svg).
Research cutoff: **24 September 2026**. New geographic artwork, not a converted network diagram.

### Scope and line histories

Includes Lines 1, 2, 3 (including its airport branch), 4, 5, 6 (including the Chongqing East branch),
9, 10, 18, the Loop Line, International Expo Line, Jiangtiao, Bitong and the simplified Bishan
SkyShuttle. The Expo branch has its own legend identity, using its longstanding 国博线 name.
The 3 and 6 branches retain their parent line identity. Through trains on 4/Loop/5 and 5/Jiangtiao
do not duplicate the underlying infrastructure.

Public passenger previews count. Temporary maintenance, accident, epidemic and integration-test
suspensions do not erase built lines. No never-opened stations or proposed extensions are drawn.
This is the rail-transit network, not every historical transport mode in Chongqing: the separate
river cableways, funiculars, escalators, national railways and scenic park rides are outside scope.

The following secondary line histories and their station tables were read as an inventory and
cross-checked with dated announcements. They also index intermediate stations not named in the
short event descriptions. Recovered Weibo/WeChat citations are identified explicitly; recovering
a citation does not mean the original post was independently retrievable.

- [Line 1](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/重庆轨道交通1号线).
- [Line 2](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/重庆轨道交通2号线).
- [Line 3 and airport branch](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/重庆轨道交通3号线).
- [Line 4](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/重庆轨道交通4号线).
- [Line 5](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/重庆轨道交通5号线).
- [Line 6 and International Expo Line](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/重庆轨道交通6号线).
- [Line 9](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/重庆轨道交通9号线).
- [Line 10](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/重庆轨道交通10号线).
- [Line 18](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/重庆轨道交通18号线).
- [Loop Line](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/重庆轨道交通环线).
- [Jiangtiao Line](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/江跳线).
- [Bitong Line](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/璧铜线).
- [Bishan SkyShuttle](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/重庆云巴1号线).

### Date and interchange decisions

- **Line 2:** starts with paid Daping–Zoo public sightseeing on November 6, 2004, rather than
  the June 18, 2005 formal opening. Jiaochangkou follows on December 11. The dated government
  trial notice lists nine boarding stations from December 28: Jiaochangkou, Linjiangmen, Daxigou,
  Niujiaotuo, Liziba, Daping, Yuanjiagang, Yangjiaping and Zoo. Together with the five-stop November
  service, this establishes the early marker inventory. Xiejiawan remains built despite being
  omitted from the later regular trial stopping pattern. Fotuguan, Zengjiayan and Huanghuayuan
  follow their station chronologies. The often-repeated December 28 Huanghuayuan date conflicts
  with that contemporary list and is not used.
- **Gaomiaocun:** October 17, 2011, as in the Chinese station chronology and contemporary
  October 13 announcement. The English opening table's November 17 date is inconsistent.
- **Line 6 north:** December 31, 2013 adds five operating stations, including Jinshansi.
  Caojiawan, Xiangjiagang and Tiansheng open later. An opening table that counts four omitted Jinshansi.
- **Bitong:** December 26, 2024 public trials, not only January 2, 2025 formal operation.
  The [trial announcement](https://wap.cq.gov.cn/ywdt/bmts/202412/t20241224_14016273.html)
  directs passengers to exit Line 1 and enter Bitong separately; the paid-area connection is dated
  to the formal opening. Qinglonghu was not one of the eight opened stations.
- **SkyShuttle:** February 15, 2021 begins five-station public trials along the full route;
  [February 20 photographs](https://photo.sina.cn/album_35_21245_607484.htm?ch=35) and
  [Xinhua's March report](https://www.xinhuanet.com/2021-03/23/c_1127246512.htm) corroborate the
  earlier public preview, while the exact February 15 day comes from the linked chronology.
  Regular all-station operation begins [April 16](https://jtj.cq.gov.cn/sy_240/bmdt/202104/t20210416_9180242.html).
  Individual SkyShuttle stops are omitted under the shared simplified-service rule; April 16 does
  not change the drawing. Its designated Line 1 connection supplies Bishan's dated membership.
- **Paid areas:** Shapingba's Line 1, Loop and Line 9 markers remain separate until September 30,
  2022; Xiejiawan's Line 2/Loop markers merge June 17, 2023. Guanyinqiao, Xietaizi, Yangjiaping and
  Yudaishan remain outside interchanges. Xietaizi has three separate station identities after its
  delayed Line 18 opening. The Line 18 opening notice identifies Fuhualu, Olympic Sports Center,
  Baijusi and Tiaodeng as in-station transfers from opening; no invented later conversion is added.
  See the [2026 operator network map](https://jtj.cq.gov.cn/zwgk_240/zfxxgkml/gggs/tzgg/202603/W020260302622398077574.pdf)
  and the dated passage notices above. The PDF's indexed text was available; direct PDF download
  was unavailable during this audit.
- **Cutoff:** Shunshuisi Park, Xinqiao, Youdiangang, Nanping's Line 10 platform and Qinglonghu
  remain absent. Line 15 is not opened merely because its fare was published:
  [September 8 fare notice](https://fzggw.cq.gov.cn/zwgk/zfxxgkml/jgxx/jgzc/202609/t20260908_16048650.html),
  [August pre-opening safety assessment](https://jtysw.cq.gov.cn/sy_240/bmdt/202608/t20260819_15951204.html),
  [September 19 power-up report](https://www.cqnews.net/web/content_1550770814051643392.html).
  Future project completion targets are not passenger opening dates.

### Rename rollout qualifications

These are implementation-date choices supported by dated reports, not claims that every sign
changed simultaneously:

1. **Chongqing North → Chongqing North Station South Square:** April 7, 2016 starts the reported
   formal rollout. Some signs were visible on March 22; the project was scheduled to finish April 30.
   [March signage report](https://news.ifeng.com/a/20160323/48123672_0.shtml), April report above.
2. **Jiangbei Airport → Terminal 2 of Jiangbei Airport:** December 25, 2017 is the dated new map
   attestation. Secondary accounts also give December 26 or the December 28 Line 10 opening;
   the exact first day of every English sign/announcement was not established.
3. **Pingchang → Happy Valley:** January 12, 2018 is the formal rename reported on January 19.
   [New platform signs were already reported December 3, 2017](https://www.cqtitle.com/new/gd/2017/1204/16876.html),
   while other signs and announcements still used Pingchang. Proposal/approval dates in March/July
   2017 are not treated as completed passenger-name changes.
4. **Four university names:** the operator starts the batch on December 1, 2019; physical signage
   work continues into March 2020. See the [university's rollout explanation](https://dwxcbwlgzb.swu.edu.cn/info/1028/3316.htm).
   Construction names and unimplemented proposals do not create passenger-history states.

### Geography, identity and distance

- Route alignments, stop locations, riverbanks and administrative boundaries are derived from
  [OpenStreetMap](https://www.openstreetmap.org/), © OpenStreetMap contributors,
  [ODbL 1.0](https://www.openstreetmap.org/copyright), retrieved September 24, 2026.
  All layers use EPSG:32648, at 18 metres per SVG unit. River polygons preserve islands and the
  Yangtze/Jialing confluence; Sichuan beyond the Chongqing boundary uses the foreign-land fill.
  Shorelines represent the current geography, not an animated hydrological history.
- OSM route relations: 2336457, 2353578, 2341149, 9597503, 7677819, 7913722, 2768661,
  19293566, 3474487, 14004563, 8300862, 15749313, 7662492, 10176833, 17135615 and 12759727.
  Chongqing boundary: 913069. These establish geometry, not historical service dates.
- Smooth cubic curves follow mapped railway ways, with local station offsets for readable straight
  interchange capsules and clearance from lines that do not serve a station. Shapingba's platforms
  are placed along their existing tracks on a common capsule axis, avoiding artificial sharp bends.
  The drawing is geographically based, not a survey or a claim of exact platform dimensions.
- There are 44 track paths, split at historical openings rather than every station, and 326 dated
  marker versions. At the cutoff, 276 marker identities are present. Separate outside interchanges
  count separately; simplified SkyShuttle stops do not inflate the heavy-rail station count.
- Published kilometres come from opening reports and the operator group's
  [April 2026 bond prospectus, printed pages 25–26](https://static.sse.com.cn/disclosure/bond/announcement/company/c/new/2026-04-22/245126_20260422_2LAT.pdf).
  Its opening-date column contains transcription errors and is not used to override contemporary
  opening notices. Lengths are rounded to tenths. Early staged Line 1/2/3 sections are apportioned
  within published totals; those subdivisions are estimates. Line 4's west section is approximately
  9.5 km after excluding the unopened 1.267 km Shunshuisi Park interval from the 10.8 km project;
  Line 9 excludes the unopened Xinqiao tail. Construction/project lengths therefore need not equal
  the sum of drawn passenger sections. No hidden track or length is added to force a headline total. Current drawn sections sum to 575.5 km, or 590.9 km including SkyShuttle.
- [CRT logo source](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Chongqing_Rail_Transit_logo.svg): the existing
  green emblem is retained in its original proportions, with the accompanying wordmark omitted.
  The mark identifies the network; it is not newly drawn or an endorsement.

Validation: the spec checker, every event and adjacent day in forward/reverse playback, station
search (including historical names and separate same-name stations), line-filtered search,
tooltip dismissal, mobile resize, home preview and actual Bézier-to-marker clearance.
The checks validate the implementation, not the unresolved exact-day rollout questions above.
